## 1. Tables that record a booking

Two separate booking systems exist side by side, plus a Cal.com mirror and a manual session log.

**`booking_passes`** — `supabase/migrations/20260708111013_ea3424fe-8663-4eaf-b57b-d8dcf55a0000.sql:3`
A row = a credit bucket ("N sessions") issued to one student, addressed by a hashed link token. Key cols: `total_credits`, `used_credits`, `session_length_minutes`, `expires_at`, `student_id`.
Status: `active | exhausted | expired | revoked`, plus `paused` added in `20260719120000_booking_pass_paused_status.sql`. Not a booking — an entitlement.

**`booking_pass_redemptions`** — same file, line 36
A row = one actual scheduled session bought with a pass credit. Cols: `start_at`, `end_at`, `schedule_event_id`, `google_event_id`, `idempotency_key`, later `reschedule_count`, `calendar_status`, `submission_status` (`none|staged|linked|failed`, `20260720180000_booking_phase4_upload_idempotency.sql:8`).
Status: originally `booked | cancelled | failed`; `no_show` added at `20260719120000_booking_pass_paused_status.sql:36-39`.

**`single_session_bookings`** — `supabase/migrations/20260816010000_single_session_schema.sql:6`
A row = one native (non-Cal.com) 70-minute paid session with its whole Stripe lifecycle: hold → checkout → paid → calendar. Carries `stripe_checkout_session_id`, `stripe_payment_intent_id`, `amount_cents`, `gcal_event_id`, `meet_url`, `booking_token_hash`, `cancel_token_hash`, `lifecycle_version`, sweeper lease cols.
Status (`single_session_status_chk`, line ~50): `draft, checkout_open, booked, cancelled, expired, abandoned, refund_pending, refunded, refund_failed`. **`booked` is the only "this is real" state; there is no `completed`.**

**`booking_commerce_events`** — `supabase/migrations/20260810000000_booking_commerce_hub.sql:33`
An append-only provider-event ledger, unique on `(provider, provider_event_id, event_type)` — the idempotency boundary. `provider ∈ stripe|calcom|lsatu`; `event_type` is free text: `'paid'`, `'unmatched_paid'`, Cal trigger names (`BOOKING_CREATED`/`BOOKING_CANCELLED`), single-session finalize events. Not a schedule; an audit log.

**`external_booking_events`** — same file, line 48
One row per Cal.com booking (`provider_booking_id` UNIQUE, upserted), with `start_at`/`end_at`, matched `student_id`, `offer_id`, and a mirror to `google_event_id`/`schedule_event_id`. Status: text defaulting `'booked'`; the webhook only ever writes `'booked'` or `'cancelled'`.

**`schedule_events`** — `supabase/migrations/20260513111010_e4e073dd-2168-4ebe-84cc-0d2cac5ea64c.sql:3`
Google Calendar mirror for the shared admin calendar. `status` default `'confirmed'` (Google's value), `deleted_at` soft-delete. Not student-scoped — do not use it to answer "did this student book".

**`student_sessions`** — `supabase/migrations/20260424020000_create_student_sessions.sql:7`
Joshua's manual log that a tutoring session *happened*: `scheduled_at`, `duration_minutes`, `notes`, `transcript_id`. Status `scheduled | completed | cancelled | no_show`, **default `'completed'`**. Written by admins/session-note ingestion (`supabase/functions/_shared/sessionIngestCore.ts:213`), never by a webhook. This is the only table with a genuine "attended" semantic.

## 2. Webhook handlers

One function, both providers: `supabase/functions/booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts`, dispatched on `?provider=stripe|calcom` (`:349-401`).

Stripe branch:
- `refund.*` → RPC `record_single_session_refund_status` (`:200-212`).
- `checkout.session.expired` → RPC `expire_single_session_checkout` (`:214-227`).
- `checkout.session.completed` with `payment_status === 'paid'` → calls `grantStudentAccess(...)` (`:234-246`), then either:
  - `metadata.product === 'single_session'` → validates amount/currency against `booking_event_types`/`booking_price_cents`, mints a cancel token, calls `finalize_single_session_payment` (`:286-300`) which flips the row to `status='booked'` and inserts a `booking_commerce_events` row (`supabase/migrations/20260915191000_fix_single_session_commerce_offer_reference.sql:94-124`).
  - otherwise matches the payment link to a `booking_offers` row and calls `apply_booking_package_payment` (`:339`), which adds `offer.credits` to the student's newest `booking_passes` row (or creates one), sets it `active`, and logs `event_type='paid'` (`20260810000000_booking_commerce_hub.sql:80-117`). Unmatched payers get `event_type='unmatched_paid'` (`:326-335`).

Cal.com branch (`:353-400`): verifies `x-cal-signature-256`, resolves the offer by event-type slug, fuzzy-matches the student by email/name (`matchStudent`, `:60-80`), mirrors to Google Calendar when matched and booked, then upserts `external_booking_events` (status `booked` or `cancelled` from `triggerEvent === 'BOOKING_CANCELLED'`) and `booking_commerce_events`. **Cal.com bookings never touch `booking_passes` credits.**

There is no "completed" webhook from either provider.

## 3. "Has this student booked at least one session?"

The canonical read path is the edge function `supabase/functions/student-booking/index.ts` action `info` (`:110-160`), which unions:
- RPC `get_student_booking(auth_uid)` → `redemptions` (`supabase/migrations/20260720190000_booking_phase5_student_state.sql:41-53`, joining `booking_pass_redemptions` → `booking_passes` on `student_id`), and
- `single_session_bookings` filtered `.eq('user_id', userId)`,
tagging each `source: 'redemption' | 'single_session'`.

Concrete condition — a student has booked iff either holds:
```
EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM booking_pass_redemptions r
        JOIN booking_passes p ON p.id = r.booking_pass_id
        WHERE p.student_id = $uid AND r.status = 'booked')
OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM single_session_bookings
        WHERE user_id = $uid AND status = 'booked')
```
Add `external_booking_events WHERE student_id = $uid AND status='booked'` only if you want Cal.com bookings, which the client already reads separately as `external_bookings`. The client-side equivalent filter is `src/hooks/useBookingScheduleItems.ts:15` (`booking.status === 'booked'`).

Caveat: `single_session_bookings.user_id` is only set when the buyer was signed in; guest purchases match by email only, and Cal.com matching is a best-effort email/name lookup that can leave `student_id` NULL.

## 4. "Session has happened / credits used up"

There is **no attendance signal from any automated path**. Three distinct notions:

- **Time passing** — the only automatic "used" signal. `src/hooks/useBookingScheduleItems.ts:17` computes `completed = new Date(end_at ?? start_at) &lt; now` purely client-side for display; nothing is persisted.
- **Credits consumed** — `booking_passes.used_credits` increments at *redemption* (booking time), not at attendance: `redeem_booking_pass` does `used_credits = used_credits + 1, status = CASE WHEN used_credits+1 &gt;= total_credits THEN 'exhausted' ELSE 'active' END` (latest version `supabase/migrations/20260718234415_...:61-62`). Cancellation refunds the credit (`20260720160000_booking_phase2_atomic_admin.sql:105`). `get_student_booking` also lazily flips `active → exhausted`/`expired` on read (`20260720190000...:13-21`). So "credits used up" = `booking_passes.status = 'exhausted'` OR `used_credits &gt;= total_credits` — it means *fully booked*, not *fully attended*.
- **Attended** — only manual: `booking_pass_redemptions.status = 'no_show'` set by admin action `mark_no_show` (`supabase/functions/booking-pass-admin/index.ts:445-450`), and `student_sessions.status = 'completed'` written by hand or by session-note ingestion (`supabase/functions/_shared/sessionIngestCore.ts:166-213`). `single_session_bookings` has no completed/attended state at all.

## 5. Automation writing profiles access flags

**Yes, one exists — on payment only, never on booking.**
`supabase/functions/_shared/grantStudentAccess.ts:26-48`: looks up the auth user by the Stripe customer email, then `profiles.update({...all PAID_STUDENT_FLAGS: true}).eq('class_id', user.id)`. Flags listed at `:9-19` (`has_classroom_access, has_practice_access, has_drill_access, has_bootcamp_access, has_journal_access, has_waj_access, has_chat_access, has_schedule_access, has_activity_access`).

Called from exactly one place: `booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts:238`, inside `checkout.session.completed` when `payment_status === 'paid'`, wrapped in try/catch so a failure never fails the webhook. It is fire-and-forget: if the payer has no account yet it returns `{granted:false, reason:'no_account'}` and nothing retries later.

Plainly: there is **no** automation that writes profiles flags in response to a *booking* (Cal.com booking, pass redemption, single-session hold, or session completion), and **nothing ever revokes** flags automatically — by design, per the comment at `grantStudentAccess.ts:5-7`. Revocation is manual via `admin-manage-users`.</result>

Both lookups are saved. Here is what they found, in plain words.

## Good news: Tier 1 is half built

The platform already turns on 9 of the 13 switches automatically. It does this when Stripe reports a payment. The code is `grantStudentAccess.ts`.

It does not turn on 4 switches: Analytics, Flagged, Export, and TA.

But note the trigger. It fires on **payment**, not on a booking. A Cal.com booking changes no switches.

## A problem with Tier 1 expiry

I recommended that Tier 1 access must never stop by itself. The code now proves this is the only safe choice.

No system knows that a session happened. Stripe does not send a "session complete" event. Cal.com does not send one either. The Single Session record has no "complete" state at all.

Only you know. You mark it by hand, or the session notes do it.

So a rule like "access stops after the session is used" cannot be built correctly. A time rule or a manual rule are the only real options.

## Good news: Tier 3 has a working model

The Bootcamps already work the way you describe Tier 3. `BootcampGate` looks for an assigned item. It opens the Bootcamp if one exists. It shows "Not assigned yet" if none exists. There is **no switch involved**.

So Tier 3 is not new. It is the Bootcamp rule applied more widely.

## A problem with Tier 3

You gave a practice test section as your example. There are two different things here.

**A timed section can be assigned.** You build a Question Set from one PreTest section and assign it. This works today.

**But the student needs two switches to sit it.** The directions screen needs the Classroom switch. The GO button starts the Drill, which needs the Drill switch.

So a Tier 3 student with the Drill switch OFF can open the directions screen and then press a dead GO button. Assignment-only access cannot work while the runners still check switches.

**A full practice test cannot be assigned at all.** The Testing Centre has no assignment. A student starts a test alone. The Drill switch is the only control. This is the largest gap for Tier 3.

---

None of this changes the five questions. Please answer them and I will continue.

Everything in the app reads those switches today. If a tier replaces them, every gate in the product has to change. A tier that sets them changes nothing downstream, and Tier 2 is then simply "the switches you picked."

➡️ **The tier sets the switches.** Add one field that records which tier a student is on. The switches stay as the thing the app actually checks. This also makes your instant-toggle requirement free, because flag changes already reach an open tab immediately.

❓ **Q7** — **Your two answers disagree about Messages**: On Q3 you said a new student gets "just the homepage, and to be able to schedule" — two things. On Q5 you approved my floor, which was three things: homepage, **messages**, and schedule. I will not pick for you.

➡️ **Include Messages.** When a student hits a locked door, the screen tells them to message you. With Messages off that is a dead end, and a new enrollee has no way to ask why they cannot get in. Schedule alone lets them book but not speak to you.

❓ **Q8** — **What did you click?**: You said "when I clicked tier two, it should have given me tier two." Nothing in the code I merged today has a tier control. Did you ask the Lovable agent to build tiers separately, or were you describing what you expect to happen?

For your Single Session flow these are effectively the same moment, because payment is what marks it booked. A Cal.com booking would not trigger it.

Also: the existing code turns on 9 switches, not 13. It leaves off Analytics, Flagged, Export and TA.

➡️ **Keep payment as the trigger, and raise it to all 13 switches.** Money is the honest signal, and it is already wired. Tier 1 should mean everything, with no quiet exceptions. Cal.com-only bookings stay a manual case.

❓ **Q10** — **The Tier 3 problem that has no clean answer yet**: A Tier 3 student has Drill off. You assign a timed section. They open the directions screen, press GO, and hit a dead end, because the runner checks the Drill switch. Assignment-only access cannot work while the runners check switches.

Two ways out. (a) Follow the Bootcamp pattern: an assigned item authorises its own runner, so assigned work opens regardless of switches. (b) Turn on whichever switches the assigned work needs, which quietly gives them the whole Drill.

➡️ **(a), the Bootcamp pattern.** It already works this way for Bootcamps, so it is proven in your product rather than new. ## What we agreed

**A tier is a template.** It sets the switches; the switches stay what the app actually checks. One tier per student.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Tier 1** | Everything on. You toggle it yourself. Never expires. |
| **Tier 2** | You pick each feature. Instant. No memory. |
| **Tier 3** | Only what you assign, plus the floor. |
| **(no tier)** | Undecided. Same access as Tier 3, but sitting in your queue. |

The floor is Classroom home, Messages, Schedule. Nothing else.

**Tier 1 is all-on by definition**, so turning one switch off relabels them Tier 2. Only the label moves, never the access.

**The spine of Tier 3: per item, never per feature.** Assigning one practice test does not hand over the Testing Centre. Assigning one drill does not hand over the question bank. Assigned work authorises its own runner, the way Bootcamps already do.

**Your rule, stated twice: access never changes by itself.** So the payment automation that currently grants 9 switches behind your back gets removed and becomes a notification.

**The signup queue** sits on both Today and Students, holds new signups and anyone with no tier. Checkmark assigns a tier; X puts them on Tier 3, which is functionally no access and clears them from the queue.

**12 switches, not 13** — the two Wrong answer switches merge. Four were lying about what they control and get rewired, with everyone's effective access held identical through the change.

**Migration day:** 19 → Tier 1, 6 → Tier 2 with their toggles kept, 14 → Tier 3. Those 14 regain Messages and Schedule. That's the one deliberate widening, and it also closes the loose end from this morning, so don't handle them separately.

**Not doing:** students never see tier names, no change history, no link to pricing or session length.

## Two things worth flagging before you approve

**This is a big build.** Roughly: a new tier column and template logic; rewiring four switches plus a data migration that preserves access; per-item authorisation in four separate runners; a brand-new assignment path for full practice tests, which has no table today; the signup queue on two screens; and removing the payment grant. That's several sessions, not one.

**The full practice test is the expensive piece.** Everything else is rewiring things that exist. Per-test assignment is genuinely new. 1. **Rewire the switches.** Fix the four that lie, merge the journal pair, 13 → 12. Migration preserves everyone's access exactly.
2. **Tier column and templates.** `access_tier` on profiles, the drift rule as a database trigger so it holds no matter what writes, tier control on the Students page.

6. **Per-item authorisation.** The `AssignmentGate`, across all six routes. This is what makes Tier 3 mean what you said.
7. **Assignable practice tests.** The only piece with no existing foundation.

Each stage ships and reverts on its own.

## Three things in there you should look at specifically

**Stages 4 and 5 must ship together.** If payment stops granting access before the queue exists, someone who pays on a Saturday night sits locked out with nothing on your screen telling you. I've written that as a hard rule rather than a preference.

# Session Handoff — LSAT U student access: fixed a broken revoke, added archive/delete, designed a three-tier access model

## Where it started
Joshua asked how the platform's student access control currently works, wanting a better on/off toggle. Investigation found "Revoke All Access" did not actually revoke all access. That expanded into shipping two fixes, then a full `/grill-me` interview designing a three-tier access system, ending in a written implementation plan awaiting sign-off.

## Decisions locked + what shipped

- **PR #129 — revoke actually revokes** (merged to main, `b047193`). Six gaps closed: `has_classroom_access` was force-kept true; `/guided/:assignmentId` and `/guided-drills/:slug` had no guard at all; `/conditional-indicators-refresher` and `/classroom/uploads` admitted any signed-in user; `has_activity_access` was missing from the DB guard trigger so students could self-grant it; permissions cached 5 min with no invalidation.
- **`profiles` added to `supabase_realtime`** so a flag change reaches an open tab immediately. Migration `20260922120000_profiles_realtime_and_activity_guard.sql`. Applied to the live DB via the Lovable agent.
- **PR #130 — archive + permanent delete** (merged to main, `dbeeac5`). Archive: all flags off, `archived_at` stamped, hidden from list and count, reversible. Delete: removes auth user, requires typed email match, primary admin protected, self-delete blocked. `student_sessions.student_id` changed from NOT NULL/CASCADE to nullable/SET NULL so Joshua's session records survive a delete; `deleted_students` table + BEFORE DELETE trigger snapshots identity and backfills `former_student_id`.
- **Dropped a planned `auth.admin.signOut(class_id)` call** mid-build — that API takes a JWT, not a user id, so it would have silently failed on every archive. UI copy corrected to match.
- **Tier design settled** across 8 interview rounds. A tier is a template that sets the switches; switches remain enforcement. 12 switches, not 13. Tier 1 = all on, manual, indefinite, relabels to Tier 2 on any deviation. Tier 2 = individual toggles, persist as normal state, no snapshot restored. Tier 3 = floor (Classroom/Messages/Schedule) + assigned items only. Governing rule, stated twice by Joshua: **access never changes by itself**. Per item, never per feature.
- **Plan written**, pushed to branch `docs/student-access-tiers-plan` (`0d01fa9`), NOT merged — awaiting sign-off.

## Key files for next session
- Plan file: `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\plans\student-access-tiers.md` — read first. Seven stages, risks, open items.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\brainstorms\2026-09-22-student-access-tiers.md` — full interview record, including recommendations Joshua overrode and why.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\teacher\desk\DeskPeopleSurface.tsx` — the People page; archive/delete/revoke UI.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\supabase\functions\admin-manage-users\index.ts` — all access actions.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\hooks\useUserPermissions.ts` — flag read + realtime invalidation.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\hub\BootcampGate.tsx` — the per-item gate pattern Stage 6 copies.
- Memory files touched: none.

## Running state
- Background processes: none. Two Explore sub-agents were dispatched and both completed.
